Charmless Semileptonic Baryonic B u , d , s B_{u,d,s} Decays
Chun-Khiang Chua

Abstract
We study B ¯ q → 𝐁 ​ 𝐁 ¯ ′ ​ l ​ ν ¯ \overline{B}_{q}\to{{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}}^{\prime}l\bar{\nu} and B ¯ q → 𝐁 ​ 𝐁 ¯ ′ ​ ν ​ ν ¯ \overline{B}_{q}\to{{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}}^{\prime}\nu\bar{\nu} decays with all low lying octet ( ℬ {\cal B} ) and decuplet ( 𝒟 {\cal D} ) baryons using a topological amplitude approach. In tree induced B ¯ q → 𝐁 ​ 𝐁 ¯ ′ ​ l ​ ν ¯ \overline{B}_{q}\to{{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}}^{\prime}l\bar{\nu} decay modes, we need two tree amplitudes and one annihilation amplitude in B ¯ q → ℬ ​ ℬ ¯ ′ ↕ ν ¯ \overline{B}_{q}\to{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}^{\prime}l\bar{\nu} decays, one tree amplitude in B ¯ q → ℬ ​ 𝒟 ¯ ↕ ν ¯ \overline{B}_{q}\to{\cal B}\overline{\cal D}l\bar{\nu} decays, one tree amplitude in B ¯ q → 𝒟 ​ ℬ ¯ ↕ ν ¯ \overline{B}_{q}\to{\cal D}\overline{\cal B}l\bar{\nu} decays and one tree amplitude and one annihilation amplitude in B ¯ q → 𝒟 ​ 𝒟 ¯ ′ ↕ ν ¯ \overline{B}_{q}\to{\cal D}\overline{\cal D}^{\prime}l\bar{\nu} decays. In loop induced B ¯ q → 𝐁 ​ 𝐁 ¯ ′ ​ ν ​ ν ¯ \overline{B}_{q}\to{{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}}^{\prime}\nu\bar{\nu} decay modes, similar numbers of penguin-box and penguin-box-annihilation amplitudes are needed. As the numbers of independent topological amplitudes are highly limited, there are plenty of relations on these semileptonic baryonic B q B_{q} decay amplitudes. Furthermore, the loop topological amplitudes and tree topological amplitudes have simple relations, as their ratios are fixed by known CKM factors and loop functions. It is observed that the B − → p ​ p ¯ ​ μ − ​ ν ¯ B^{-}\to p\bar{p}\mu^{-}\bar{\nu} differential rate exhibits threshold enhancement, which is expected to hold in all other semileptonic baryonic modes. The threshold enhancement effectively squeezes the phase space toward the threshold region and leads to very large SU(3) breaking effects in the decay rates. They are estimated using the measured B − → p ​ p ¯ ​ μ − ​ ν ¯ B^{-}\to p\bar{p}\mu^{-}\bar{\nu} differential rate and model calculations. From the model calculations, we find that branching ratios of non-annihilation B ¯ q → 𝐁 ​ 𝐁 ¯ ′ ​ l ​ ν ¯ \overline{B}_{q}\to{{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}}^{\prime}l\bar{\nu} modes are of the orders of 10 − 9 ∼ 10 − 6 10^{-9}\sim 10^{-6} , while branching ratios of non-penguin-box-annihilation B ¯ q → 𝐁 ​ 𝐁 ¯ ′ ​ ν ​ ν ¯ \overline{B}_{q}\to{{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}}^{\prime}\nu\bar{\nu} modes are of the orders of 10 − 12 ∼ 10 − 8 10^{-12}\sim 10^{-8} . Modes with relatively unsuppressed rates and good detectability are identified. These modes can be searched experimentally in near future and the rate estimations can be improved when more modes are discovered. Ratios of rates of some loop induced B ¯ q → 𝐁 ​ 𝐁 ¯ ′ ​ ν ​ ν ¯ \overline{B}_{q}\to{{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}}^{\prime}\nu\bar{\nu} decays and tree induced B ¯ q → 𝐁 ​ 𝐁 ¯ ′ ​ l ​ ν ¯ \overline{B}_{q}\to{{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}}^{\prime}l\bar{\nu} decays are predicted and can be checked experimentally. They can be tests of the SM. Some implications on B ¯ q → 𝐁 ​ 𝐁 ¯ ′ ​ 𝐥 + ​ 𝐥 − \overline{B}_{q}\to{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}^{\prime}l^{+}l^{-} decays are also given.



pacs
11.30.Hv, 13.25.Hw, 14.40.Nd

I
Introduction

Recently, there are some experimental activities on
\overline{B}\to{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}^{\prime}l\bar{\nu}
and
\overline{B}\to{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}^{\prime}\nu\bar{\nu}
decays, where
{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}^{\prime}
are baryon anti-baryon pairs
CLEO:2003bit
;
Belle:2013uqr
;
LHCb:2019cgl
;
PDG
;
BaBar:2019awu
.
The present experimental results are summarized in Table
1
.
In particular, the branching ratio of
B^{-}\to p\bar{p}\mu^{-}\bar{\nu}_{\mu}
decay is measured to be
(5.27^{+0.23}_{-0.24}\pm 0.21\pm 0.15)\times 10^{-6}

by LHCb
LHCb:2019cgl
and
Br(B^{-}\to p\bar{p}l\bar{\nu})=(5.8^{+2.6}_{-2.3})\times 10^{-6}
by Belle
Belle:2013uqr
(see also
PDG
),
while only upper limit of
Br(B^{-}\to\Lambda\bar{p}\nu\bar{\nu})<3.0\times 10^{-5}
was reported by BaBar
BaBar:2019awu
.

Theoretically, the branching ratios of
\overline{B}\to{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}^{\prime}l\bar{\nu}
decays were estimated and predicted to be of the order of
10^{-6}
to
10^{-4}

Hou:2000bz
;
Geng:2011tr
.
Some recent studies are devoted to understand the rate of the
B^{-}\to p\bar{p}l\bar{\nu}
decay
Geng:2021sdl
;
Hsiao:2022uzx
as the measured rate is roughly 20 times smaller than a previous theoretical prediction
Geng:2011tr
, while the shape of the predicted differential rate using QCD counting rules agrees well with data, which exhibits threshold enhancement
Geng:2011tr
;
LHCb:2019cgl
.
In this work we will employ the approach of refs.
Chua:2003it
;
Chua:2013zga
;
Chua:2016aqy
;
Chua:2022wmr
,
which was used to study two-body baryonic
B
decays,
\overline{B}\to{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}^{\prime}
, making use of the well established topological amplitude formalism
Zeppenfeld:1980ex
;
Chau:tk
;
Chau:1990ay
;
Gronau:1994rj
;
Gronau:1995hn
;
Chiang:2004nm
;
Cheng:2014rfa
;
Savage:ub
;
He:2018php
;
He:2018joe
;
Wang:2020gmn
.
The decay amplitudes of
\overline{B}\to{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}^{\prime}l\bar{\nu}
and
\overline{B}\to{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}^{\prime}\nu\bar{\nu}
decays with all low lying octet (
{\cal B}
) and decuplet (
{\cal D}
) baryons will be decomposed into combinations of several topological amplitudes.
As the numbers of topological amplitudes are highly limited, there are many relations of decay amplitudes.

It is well known that a decay rate strongly depends on the masses of the final state particles when the decay is just above the threshold.
The rates may vary in orders of magnitudes even if the amplitudes are of similar sizes.
One normally does not expect such behavior in
B_{q}
decays when large phase spaces are available.
From the experimental differential rate
dBr/dm_{p\bar{p}}
of
B^{-}\to p\bar{p}\mu^{-}\bar{\nu}
decay from LHCb
LHCb:2019cgl
as shown in
Fig.
1
, one can easily see that the spectrum exhibits prominent threshold enhancement, which is a comment feature in three or more body baryonic
B_{q}
decays
Hou:2000bz
;
Chua:2001vh
;
Cheng:2001tr
;
Chua:2002wn
;
Chua:2002yd
;
Geng:2011tr
;
Huang:2021qld
;
Geng:2021sdl
;
Hsiao:2022uzx
.
Threshold enhancement is expected to hold in all other semileptonic baryonic modes considered in this work as well.
The threshold enhancement effectively squeezes the phase space to the threshold region, see Fig.
1
, and thus mimics the decay just above threshold situation.
Consequently, it amplifies the effects of SU(3) breaking in final state baryon masses and
can lead to very large SU(3) breaking effects in the decay rates.
The SU(3) breakings in the decay rates from threshold enhancements will be estimated using the measured
B^{-}\to p\bar{p}\mu^{-}\bar{\nu}
differential rate and model calculations with available theoretical inputs from refs.
Geng:2021sdl
;
Hsiao:2022uzx
, which can reproduce the measured
B^{-}\to p\bar{p}\mu^{-}\bar{\nu}
differential rate.

and
{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}^{\prime}\nu\bar{\nu}
branching ratios. The upper limits are at 90% confidence level.

(8.2^{+3.7}_{-3.2}\pm 0.6)\times 10^{-6}

(8.2^{+4.0}_{-3.3})\times 10^{-6}

PDG
PDG

B^{-}\to p\bar{p}\mu^{-}\bar{\nu}_{\mu}

(3.1^{+3.1}_{-2.4}\pm 0.7)\times 10^{-6}

Belle
Belle:2013uqr

(5.27^{+0.23}_{-0.24}\pm 0.21\pm 0.15)\times 10^{-6}

LHCb
LHCb:2019cgl

(5.32\pm 0.34)\times 10^{-6}

PDG
PDG

B^{-}\to p\bar{p}l\bar{\nu}\,(l=e,\mu)

(5.8^{+2.6}_{-2.3})\times 10^{-6}

Belle
Belle:2013uqr
, PDG
PDG

B^{-}\to\Lambda\bar{p}\nu\bar{\nu}

(0.4\pm 1.1\pm 0.6)\times 10^{-5}

(<3.0\times 10^{-5})

BaBar
BaBar:2019awu

We will try to identify modes with relatively unsuppressed rates and good detectability.
The estimation on rates can be improved when more modes are discovered.
Recently hints of new physics effects in rare
B
decays are accumulating,
see, for example,
Altmannshofer:2021qrr
;
Cornella:2021sby
;
Geng:2021nhg
.
Given the present situation and the fact that
\overline{B}\to{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}^{\prime}l\bar{\nu}
decays are tree induced decay modes, while
\overline{B}\to{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}^{\prime}\nu\bar{\nu}
decays are loop induced decay modes,
it will be interesting and useful to identify
\overline{B}\to{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}^{\prime}\nu\bar{\nu}
and
\overline{B}\to{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}^{\prime}l\bar{\nu}
decay modes which have good detectability.
Their rate ratios, especially, those insensitive to the modeling of SU(3) breaking from threshold enhancement, can be tests of the Standard Model (SM).

Figure 1:

The experimental differential rate
dBr/dm_{p\bar{p}}
of
B^{-}\to p\bar{p}\mu^{-}\bar{\nu}
decay from LHCb
LHCb:2019cgl
exhibits threshold enhancement.
The threshold enhancement effectively squeezes the phase space toward the threshold region.

The layout of this paper is as following.
We give the formalism for decomposing amplitudes in terms of topological amplitudes and modeling of the topological amplitudes in Sec. II.
In Sec. III, results on decay amplitudes in term of topological amplitudes, relations of decay amplitudes and decay rates are provided.
Conclusion and discussions are given in Sec. IV, where some comments on
\overline{B}_{q}\to{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}^{\prime}l^{+}l^{-}
decays will also be given.
Appendix A concerning the transition matrix elements in the asymptotic limit and Appendix B with some useful formulas for calculating 4-body decay rates are added at the end of the paper.

II
Formalism

II.1
Topological amplitudes

The decay amplitudes of
\overline{B}_{q}\to{\bf B}\overline{\bf B}^{\prime}l\bar{\nu}
and
\overline{B}_{q}\to{\bf B}\overline{\bf B}^{\prime}\nu\bar{\nu}
decays are given by
Geng:2011tr
;
Geng:2012qn

\displaystyle A(\overline{B}_{q}\to{\bf B}\overline{\bf B}^{\prime}l\bar{\nu})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{G_{F}}{\sqrt{2}}V_{ub}\langle{\bf B}\overline{\bf B}^{\prime}|\bar{u}_{L}\gamma_{\mu}b_{L}|\overline{B}_{q}\rangle\bar{l}_{L}\gamma^{\mu}\nu_{L},

\displaystyle A(\overline{B}_{q}\to{\bf B}\overline{\bf B}^{\prime}\nu\bar{\nu})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{G_{F}}{\sqrt{2}}\frac{\alpha_{\rm em}}{2\pi\sin^{2}\theta_{W}}V^{*}_{ts}V_{tb}D(m_{t}^{2}/m_{W}^{2})\langle{\bf B}\overline{\bf B}^{\prime}|\bar{s}_{L}\gamma_{\mu}b_{L}|\overline{B}_{q}\rangle\bar{\nu}_{L}\gamma^{\mu}\nu_{L},

(1)

where
V_{ub}
,
V_{ts}
and
V_{tb}
are Cabibbo-Kobayashi-Maskawa (CKM) matrix elements,

D(x)
,
D_{0}(x)
and
D_{1}(x)
are loop functions with
loopfunctions

\displaystyle D(x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle D_{0}(x)+\frac{\alpha_{s}}{4\pi}D_{1}(x),

\displaystyle D_{0}(x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{x}{8}\bigg(-\frac{2+x}{1-x}+\frac{3x-6}{(1-x)^{2}}\ln x\bigg),

\displaystyle D_{1}(x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{23x+5x^{2}-4x^{3}}{3(1-x)^{2}}+\frac{x-11x^{2}+x^{3}+x^{4}}{(1-x)^{3}}\ln x+\frac{8x+4x^{2}+x^{3}-x^{4}}{2(1-x)^{3}}\ln^{2}x

(2)

\displaystyle-\frac{4x-x^{3}}{(1-x)^{2}}Li_{2}(1-x)+8x\frac{dD_{0}(x)}{dx}\ln\frac{\mu^{2}}{m_{W}^{2}}.

Note that the
\overline{B}_{q}\to{\bf B}\overline{\bf B}^{\prime}l\bar{\nu}
decay is governed by the matrix element,
\langle{\bf B}\overline{\bf B}^{\prime}|\bar{u}_{L}\gamma_{\mu}b_{L}|\overline{B}_{q}\rangle
, while the
\overline{B}_{q}\to{\bf B}\overline{\bf B}^{\prime}\nu\bar{\nu}
decay is governed by the matrix element,
\langle{\bf B}\overline{\bf B}^{\prime}|\bar{s}_{L}\gamma_{\mu}b_{L}|\overline{B}_{q}\rangle
.
These two matrix elements are difficult to calculate as they involve baryon pairs
{\bf B}\overline{\bf B}^{\prime}
in the final state.
Nevertheless they are related by interchanging
u
and
s
and, hence, can be related by SU(3) transformations.

Figure 2:
Topological diagrams of
(a)
T
(tree) and (b)
A
(annihilation) amplitudes in
\overline{B}_{q}\to{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}^{\prime}l\bar{\nu}
decays and (c)
PB
(penguin and box) and (d)
PBA
(penguin-box annihilation) amplitudes in
\overline{B}_{q}\to{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}^{\prime}\nu\bar{\nu}
decays.
These are flavor flow diagrams, where gluon lines are not shown.

It is known that topological amplitude approach is related to SU(3) approach
Zeppenfeld:1980ex
;
Gronau:1994rj
;
Savage:ub
.
We follow the approach similar to the one employed in the study of
\overline{B}_{q}\to{\bf B}\overline{\bf B}^{\prime}
decays
Chua:2003it
;
Chua:2013zga
;
Chua:2016aqy
;
Chua:2022wmr
to decompose
\overline{B}_{q}\to{\bf B}\overline{\bf B}^{\prime}l\bar{\nu}
and
{\bf B}\overline{\bf B}^{\prime}\nu\bar{\nu}
decay amplitudes
into topological amplitudes.

From Eq. (
1
), we see that the
Hamiltonian governing

\overline{B}_{q}\to{\bf B}\overline{\bf B}^{\prime}l\bar{\nu}
decays
has the following flavor structure,

\displaystyle(\bar{u}b)=H^{i}_{T}(\bar{q}_{i}b),

(3)

with

\displaystyle H^{1}_{T}=1,\quad{\rm otherwise}\,\,\,H^{i}_{T}=0,

(4)

where we take
q_{1,2,3}=u,d,s
as usual.
Similarly, the
Hamiltonian governing

\overline{B}_{q}\to{\bf B}\overline{\bf B}^{\prime}\nu\bar{\nu}
decays
has the following flavor structure,

\displaystyle(\bar{s}b)=H^{k}_{PB}(\bar{q}_{k}b),

(5)

with

\displaystyle H^{3}_{PB}=1,\quad{\rm otherwise}\,\,\,H^{k}_{PB}=0.

(6)

These
H_{T}
and
H_{PB}
will be used as spurion fields in the following constructions of effective Hamiltonian,
H_{\rm eff}
.

We shall start with
\overline{B}_{q}\to{{\cal D}\overline{\cal D}}^{\prime}l\bar{\nu}
decays with
{\cal D}
the low-lying decuplet baryon.
The flavor flow diagram for a
\overline{B}_{q}\to{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}^{\prime}l\bar{\nu}
decay is given in Fig.
2
.
Note that in the case of a
\overline{B}_{q}\to{{\cal D}\overline{\cal D}}^{\prime}l\bar{\nu}
decay,
the
q_{i}q_{j}q_{l}
and
\bar{q}^{l}\bar{q}^{j}\bar{q}^{m}
flavors as
shown in Fig.
2
correspond to the following fields,

\displaystyle q_{i}q_{j}q_{l}\to\overline{\cal D}_{ijl},\quad\bar{q}^{l}\bar{q}^{j}\bar{q}^{m}\to{\cal D}^{jlm},

(7)

in the Hamiltonian, respectively,
where
{\cal D}^{jlm}
denotes
the familiar decuplet field, and, explicitly, we have

{\cal D}^{111}=\Delta^{++}
,

{\cal D}^{112}=\Delta^{+}/\sqrt{3}
,

{\cal D}^{122}=\Delta^{0}/\sqrt{3}
,

{\cal D}^{222}=\Delta^{-}
,

{\cal D}^{113}=\Sigma^{*-}/\sqrt{3}
,

{\cal D}^{123}=\Sigma^{*0}/\sqrt{6}
,

{\cal D}^{223}=\Sigma^{*-}/\sqrt{3}
,

{\cal D}^{133}=\Xi^{*0}/\sqrt{3}
,

{\cal D}^{233}=\Xi^{*-}/\sqrt{3}

and
{\cal D}^{333}=\Omega^{-}

(see, for example
text
).
By using the above correspondent rule,
we obtain the following effective Hamiltonian for
\overline{B}_{q}\to{{\cal D}\overline{\cal D}}^{\prime}l\bar{\nu}
decays,

\displaystyle H_{\rm eff}(\overline{B}_{q}\to{{\cal D}\overline{\cal D}}l\bar{\nu})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 6\,T_{{\cal D}\overline{\cal D}}\,\overline{B}_{m}H^{i}_{T}\overline{\cal D}_{ijl}{\cal D}^{ljm}+A_{{\cal D}\overline{\cal D}}\,\overline{B}_{i}H^{i}_{T}\overline{\cal D}_{mjl}{\cal D}^{ljm},

(8)

with
{\overline{B}}_{m}=\left(B^{-},\overline{B}{}^{0},\overline{B}{}^{0}_{s}\right)
.
Without lost of generality,
the pre-factors are assigned for
latter purpose.

For the
\overline{B}_{q}\to{\cal D}\overline{\cal B}l\bar{\nu}
decays,
we note that the anti-octet final state is produced by the

{\cal B}^{j}_{k}
field with
text

\displaystyle{\mathcal{B}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}{{\Sigma^{0}}\over\sqrt{2}}+{{\Lambda}\over\sqrt{6}}&{\Sigma^{+}}&{p}\\
{\Sigma^{-}}&-{{\Sigma^{0}}\over\sqrt{2}}+{{\Lambda}\over\sqrt{6}}&{n}\\
{\Xi^{-}}&{\Xi^{0}}&-\sqrt{2\over 3}{\Lambda}\end{array}\right),

where
{\cal B}^{j}_{k}

has the following flavor structure
q^{j}q^{a}q^{b}\epsilon_{abk}-\frac{1}{3}\,\delta^{j}_{k}q^{c}q^{a}q^{b}

text
. To match the flavor of
\bar{q}^{l}\bar{q}^{j}\bar{q}^{m}
in the final state as shown in Fig.
2
,
we use

\displaystyle\bar{q}^{l}\bar{q}^{j}\bar{q}^{m}\to\epsilon^{ljb}{\cal B}^{m}_{b},\,\ \epsilon^{lbm}{\cal B}^{j}_{b},\,\ \epsilon^{bjm}{\cal B}^{l}_{b},

(13)

which are, however, not totally independent, as
it can be easily shown that they are subjected to the following relation,

\displaystyle\epsilon^{ljb}{\cal B}^{m}_{b}+\epsilon^{lbm}{\cal B}^{j}_{b}+\epsilon^{bjm}{\cal B}^{l}_{b}=0.

(14)

Hence we only need two of the terms in the right-hand-side of Eq. (
13
), and, without loss of generality, the first two terms are chosen.
The effective Hamiltonian of the
\overline{B}\to{\cal D}\overline{\cal B}l\bar{\nu}
decays can be obtained by
replacing
{\cal D}^{ljm}
in Eq. (
8
) by

({\cal B}_{1})^{ljm}\equiv\epsilon^{ljb}{\cal B}^{m}_{b}
and

({\cal B}_{2})^{ljm}\equiv\epsilon^{bjm}{\cal B}^{l}_{b}
,
and, consequently, we have

\displaystyle H_{\rm eff}(\overline{B}_{q}\to{{\cal D}\overline{\cal B}}l\bar{\nu})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sqrt{6}\,T_{1{{\cal D}\overline{\cal B}}}\,\overline{B}_{m}H^{i}_{T}\overline{\cal D}_{ijl}\epsilon^{ljb}{\cal B}^{m}_{b}+\sqrt{6}\,T_{2{{\cal D}\overline{\cal B}}}\,\overline{B}_{m}H^{i}_{T}\overline{\cal D}_{ijl}\epsilon^{bjm}{\cal B}^{l}_{b}

(15)

\displaystyle+\sqrt{6}\,A_{1{{\cal D}\overline{\cal B}}}\,\overline{B}_{i}H^{i}_{T}\overline{\cal D}_{mjl}\epsilon^{ljb}{\cal B}^{m}_{b}+\sqrt{6}\,A_{2{{\cal D}\overline{\cal B}}}\,\overline{B}_{i}H^{i}_{T}\overline{\cal D}_{mjl}\epsilon^{bjm}{\cal B}^{l}_{b},

where some pre-factors are introduced without lost of generality.
Note that the
T_{1{{\cal D}\overline{\cal B}}}
,
A_{1{{\cal D}\overline{\cal B}}}
and
A_{2{{\cal D}\overline{\cal B}}}
terms are vanishing and we only have

\displaystyle H_{\rm eff}(\overline{B}_{q}\to{{\cal D}\overline{\cal B}}l\bar{\nu})=\sqrt{6}\,T_{{{\cal D}\overline{\cal B}}}\,\overline{B}_{m}H^{i}_{T}\overline{\cal D}_{ijl}\epsilon^{bjm}{\cal B}^{l}_{b},

(16)

with
T_{2{{\cal D}\overline{\cal B}}}
relabeled to
T_{{{\cal D}\overline{\cal B}}}
.

Similarly for
\overline{B}\to{{\cal B}\overline{\cal D}}l\bar{\nu}
decays,
the

q_{i}q_{k}q_{l}
flavor in the final state corresponds to
\epsilon_{ika}\overline{\cal B}^{a}_{l}
,

\epsilon_{ial}\overline{\cal B}^{a}_{k}
and
\epsilon_{akl}\overline{\cal B}^{a}_{i}
, while the last one is redundant,
since it can be expressed by the formers using the following relation,

\epsilon_{ika}\overline{\cal B}^{a}_{l}+\epsilon_{ial}\overline{\cal B}^{a}_{k}+\epsilon_{akl}\overline{\cal B}^{a}_{i}=0
.
Hence we replace the
\overline{\cal D}_{ijl}
in Eq. (
8
) by

(\overline{\cal B}_{1})_{ijl}\equiv\epsilon_{ija}\overline{\cal B}^{a}_{l}
and

(\overline{\cal B}_{2})_{ijl}\equiv\epsilon_{ajl}\overline{\cal B}^{a}_{i}

and
obtain

\displaystyle H_{\rm eff}(\overline{B}_{q}\to{{\cal B}\overline{\cal D}}l\bar{\nu})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\sqrt{6}\,T_{1{{\cal B}\overline{\cal D}}}\,\overline{B}_{m}H^{i}_{T}\epsilon_{ija}\overline{\cal B}^{a}_{l}{\cal D}^{ljm}-\sqrt{6}\,T_{2{{\cal B}\overline{\cal D}}}\,\overline{B}_{m}H^{i}\epsilon_{ajl}\overline{\cal B}^{a}_{i}{\cal D}^{ljm}

(17)

\displaystyle-\sqrt{6}\,A_{1{{\cal B}\overline{\cal D}}}\,\overline{B}_{i}H^{i}_{T}\epsilon_{mja}\overline{\cal B}^{a}_{l}{\cal D}^{ljm}-\sqrt{6}\,A_{2{{\cal B}\overline{\cal D}}}\,\overline{B}_{i}H^{i}\epsilon_{ajl}\overline{\cal B}^{a}_{m}{\cal D}^{ljm}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\sqrt{6}\,T_{{{\cal B}\overline{\cal D}}}\,\overline{B}_{m}H^{i}_{T}\epsilon_{ija}\overline{\cal B}^{a}_{l}{\cal D}^{ljm}

where the
T_{2{{\cal B}\overline{\cal D}}}
,
A_{1{{\cal B}\overline{\cal D}}}
and
A_{2{{\cal B}\overline{\cal D}}}
terms in the equation are vanishing as

\epsilon_{ajl}{\cal D}^{ljm}=\epsilon_{ajl}{\cal D}^{ljm}=0
,
and
T_{1{{\cal B}\overline{\cal D}}}
is relabeled to
T_{{{\cal B}\overline{\cal D}}}
in the last step.

To obtain the effective Hamiltonian of
\overline{B}_{q}\to{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}l\bar{\nu}
decays, we first replace

\overline{\cal D}_{ijl}
and
{\cal D}^{ljm}
in Eq. (
8
) by

(\overline{\cal B}_{1})_{ijl}\equiv\epsilon_{ija}\overline{\cal B}^{a}_{l}
,
(\overline{\cal B}_{2})_{ijl}\equiv\epsilon_{akl}\overline{\cal B}^{a}_{i}

and

({\cal B}_{1})^{lim}\equiv\epsilon^{ljb}{\cal B}^{m}_{b}
,
({\cal B}_{2})^{lim}\equiv\epsilon^{bjm}{\cal B}^{l}_{b}
,
respectively, and obtain

\displaystyle H_{\rm eff}(\overline{B}_{q}\to{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}l\bar{\nu})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-T_{11{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}\overline{B}_{m}H^{i}_{T}(\overline{\cal B}_{1})_{ijl}({\cal B}_{1})^{ljm}-T_{12{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}\overline{B}_{m}H^{i}_{T}(\overline{\cal B}_{1})_{ijl}({\cal B}_{2})^{ljm}

(18)

\displaystyle-T_{21{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}\overline{B}_{m}H^{i}_{T}(\overline{\cal B}_{2})_{ijl}({\cal B}_{1})^{ljm}-T_{22{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}\overline{B}_{m}H^{i}_{T}(\overline{\cal B}_{2})_{ijl}({\cal B}_{2})^{ljm}

\displaystyle-A_{11{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}\overline{B}_{i}H^{i}_{T}(\overline{\cal B}_{1})_{mjl}({\cal B}_{1})^{ljm}-A_{12{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}\overline{B}_{i}H^{i}_{T}(\overline{\cal B}_{1})_{mjl}({\cal B}_{2})^{ljm}

\displaystyle-A_{21{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}\overline{B}_{i}H^{i}_{T}(\overline{\cal B}_{2})_{mjl}({\cal B}_{1})^{ljm}-A_{22{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}\overline{B}_{i}H^{i}_{T}(\overline{\cal B}_{2})_{mjl}({\cal B}_{2})^{ljm}.

Using the following identity

\displaystyle-2(\overline{\cal B}_{1})_{ijl}({\cal B}_{1})^{ljm}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\overline{\cal B}_{2})_{ijl}({\cal B}_{1})^{ljm}=-2(\overline{\cal B}_{2})_{ijl}({\cal B}_{2})^{ljm},

\displaystyle-2(\overline{\cal B}_{1})_{mjl}({\cal B}_{1})^{ljm}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\overline{\cal B}_{1})_{mjl}({\cal B}_{2})^{ljm}=(\overline{\cal B}_{2})_{mjl}({\cal B}_{1})^{ljm}=-2(\overline{\cal B}_{2})_{mjl}({\cal B}_{2})^{ljm},

(19)

the above Hamiltonian can be expressed as

\displaystyle H_{\rm eff}(\overline{B}_{q}\to{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}l\bar{\nu})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(-T_{11{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}+2T_{21{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}-T_{22{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}})\overline{B}_{m}H^{i}_{T}(\overline{\cal B}_{1})_{ijl}({\cal B}_{1})^{ljm}

(20)

\displaystyle-T_{12{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}\overline{B}_{m}H^{i}_{T}(\overline{\cal B}_{1})_{ijl}({\cal B}_{2})^{ljm}

\displaystyle+(A_{11{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}-2A_{12{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}-2A_{21{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}+A_{22{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}})\overline{B}_{i}H^{i}_{T}(\overline{\cal B}_{1})_{mjl}({\cal B}_{1})^{ljm}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-T_{1{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}\overline{B}_{m}H^{i}_{T}\epsilon_{ija}\overline{\cal B}^{a}_{l}\epsilon^{bjm}{\cal B}^{l}_{b}+T_{2{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}\overline{B}_{m}H^{i}_{T}\epsilon_{ija}\overline{\cal B}^{a}_{l}\epsilon^{ljb}{\cal B}^{m}_{b}

\displaystyle+A_{{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}\overline{B}_{i}H^{i}_{T}\epsilon_{mja}\overline{\cal B}^{a}_{l}\epsilon^{ljb}{\cal B}^{m}_{b},

where the topological amplitudes are redefined as following

\displaystyle T_{1{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle T_{12{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}},

\displaystyle T_{2{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle-T_{11{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}+2T_{21{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}-T_{22{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}},

\displaystyle A_{{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle A_{11{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}-2A_{12{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}-2A_{21{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}+A_{22{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}.

(21)

With this all effective Hamiltonians of
\overline{B}_{q}\to{\bf B}\overline{\bf B}^{\prime}l\bar{\nu}
decays with low-lying octet and decuplet baryons are obtained.

The effective Hamiltonian of the
\overline{B}_{q}\to{\bf B}\overline{\bf B}^{\prime}\nu\bar{\nu}
decays can be obtained similarly. We simply give the results
in the following equation,

\displaystyle H_{\rm eff}(\overline{B}_{q}\to{{\cal D}\overline{\cal D}}\nu\bar{\nu})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 6PB_{{\cal D}\overline{\cal D}}\,\overline{B}_{m}H^{k}_{PB}\overline{\cal D}_{kjl}{\cal D}^{ljm}+PBA_{{\cal D}\overline{\cal D}}\,\overline{B}_{k}H^{k}_{PB}\overline{\cal D}_{mjl}{\cal D}^{ljm},

\displaystyle H_{\rm eff}(\overline{B}_{q}\to{{\cal D}\overline{\cal B}}\nu\bar{\nu})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sqrt{6}PB_{{\cal D}\overline{\cal B}}\,\overline{B}_{m}H^{k}_{PB}\overline{\cal D}_{kjl}\epsilon^{bjm}{\cal B}^{l}_{b},

\displaystyle H_{\rm eff}(\overline{B}_{q}\to{{\cal B}\overline{\cal D}}\nu\bar{\nu})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\sqrt{6}PB_{{\cal B}\overline{\cal D}}\,\overline{B}_{m}H^{k}_{PB}\epsilon_{kja}\overline{\cal B}^{a}_{l}{\cal D}^{ljm},

\displaystyle H_{\rm eff}(\overline{B}_{q}\to{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}\nu\bar{\nu})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-PB_{1{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}\,\overline{B}_{m}H^{k}_{PB}\epsilon_{kja}\overline{\cal B}^{a}_{l}\epsilon^{bjm}{\cal B}^{l}_{b}+PB_{2{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}\,\overline{B}_{m}H^{k}_{PB}\epsilon_{kja}\overline{\cal B}^{a}_{l}\epsilon^{ljb}{\cal B}^{m}_{b}

(22)

\displaystyle+PBA_{{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}\,\overline{B}_{k}H^{k}_{PB}\epsilon_{mja}\overline{\cal B}^{a}_{l}\epsilon^{ljb}{\cal B}^{m}_{b}.

In summary the effective Hamiltonians of
\overline{B}_{q}\to{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}^{\prime}l\bar{\nu}
and
{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}^{\prime}\nu\bar{\nu}
decays for low-lying octet and decuplet baryons are obtained and are shown in
Eqs. (
8
), (
16
), (
17
), (
20
) and (
22
).
The decay amplitudes can be obtained readily by using these effective Hamiltonians.
The results of decay amplitudes in terms of these topological amplitudes and relations on the amplitudes will be given explicitly in the next section.

Before we end this section it is important to note that, as shown in Eq. (
1
), the topological amplitudes
PB
and
T
and the topological amplitudes
PBA
and
A
should be related in the following manner,

\displaystyle\zeta

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\frac{PB_{i{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}}{T_{i{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}}=\frac{PBA_{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}{A_{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}=\frac{PB_{{\cal B}\overline{\cal D}}}{T_{{\cal B}\overline{\cal D}}}=\frac{PB_{{\cal D}\overline{\cal B}}}{T_{{\cal D}\overline{\cal B}}}=\frac{PB_{{\cal D}\overline{\cal D}}}{T_{{\cal D}\overline{\cal D}}}=\frac{PBA_{{\cal D}\overline{\cal D}}}{A_{{\cal D}\overline{\cal D}}}

(23)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\alpha_{\rm em}}{2\pi\sin^{2}\theta_{W}}\frac{V_{ts}^{*}V_{tb}}{V_{ub}}D(m_{t}^{2}/m_{W}^{2}),

where numerically we use
|V_{ub}|=0.0036
and have
\zeta=-0.037e^{i\phi_{3}}
, with
\phi_{3}=(65.5^{+1.3}_{-1.1})^{\circ}
one of the unitary angle in the CKM matrix
CKMfitter
.

II.2
Modeling the topological amplitudes

In addition to the above decompositions of amplitudes in terms of topological amplitudes,
it will be useful to have some numerical results on rates. We will use the available theoretical inputs from refs
Geng:2021sdl
and
Hsiao:2022uzx
in our modeling of the topological amplitudes and we denote them as Model 1 and Model 2, respectively.
They are used as illustration and can be improved when more data are available.

In general the topological amplitudes
T_{1{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}
,
T_{2{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}
and
A_{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}
in
\overline{B}_{q}\to{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}^{\prime}l\bar{\nu}
decays can be expressed as

\displaystyle T_{i{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle i\frac{G_{F}}{\sqrt{2}}V_{ub}\bar{l}_{L}\gamma^{\mu}\nu_{L}\bar{u}(p_{{\cal B}})\{[g^{(i)}_{1}\gamma_{\mu}+ig^{(i)}_{2}\sigma_{\mu\nu}q^{\nu}+g^{(i)}_{3}q_{\mu}+g^{(i)}_{4}(p_{{\cal B}}+p_{\overline{\cal B}^{\prime}})_{\mu}+g^{(i)}_{5}(p_{{\cal B}}-p_{\overline{\cal B}^{\prime}})_{\mu}]\gamma_{5}

\displaystyle-[f^{(i)}_{1}\gamma_{\mu}+if^{(i)}_{2}\sigma_{\mu\nu}q^{\nu}+f^{(i)}_{3}q_{\mu}+f^{(i)}_{4}(p_{{\cal B}}+p_{\overline{\cal B}^{\prime}})_{\mu}+f^{(i)}_{5}(p_{{\cal B}}-p_{\overline{\cal B}^{\prime}})_{\mu}]\}v_{R}(p_{\overline{\cal B}^{\prime}}),

\displaystyle A_{{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle i\frac{G_{F}}{\sqrt{2}}V_{ub}\bar{l}_{L}\gamma^{\mu}\nu_{L}\bar{u}(p_{{\cal B}})\{[g^{(a)}_{1}\gamma_{\mu}+ig^{(a)}_{2}\sigma_{\mu\nu}q^{\nu}+g^{(a)}_{3}q_{\mu}+g^{(a)}_{4}(p_{{\cal B}}+p_{\overline{\cal B}^{\prime}})_{\mu}+g^{(a)}_{5}(p_{{\cal B}}-p_{\overline{\cal B}^{\prime}})_{\mu}]\gamma_{5}

(24)

\displaystyle-[f^{(a)}_{1}\gamma_{\mu}+if^{(a)}_{2}\sigma_{\mu\nu}q^{\nu}+f^{(a)}_{3}q_{\mu}+f^{(a)}_{4}(p_{{\cal B}}+p_{\overline{\cal B}^{\prime}})_{\mu}+f^{(a)}_{5}(p_{{\cal B}}-p_{\overline{\cal B}^{\prime}})_{\mu}]\}v_{R}(p_{\overline{\cal B}^{\prime}}),

with
q\equiv p_{B_{q}}-p_{{\rm\bf B}}-p_{\overline{\rm\bf B}^{\prime}}
,
i=1,2
,
j=1,\dots,5
, and
f^{(i)}_{j}
,
g^{(i)}_{j}
,
f^{(a)}_{j}
and
g^{(a)}_{j}
denoting form factors.
Similarly the topological amplitudes of
\overline{B}_{q}\to{{\cal B}\overline{\cal D}}l\bar{\nu}
and
\overline{B}_{q}\to{{\cal D}\overline{\cal B}}l\bar{\nu}
decays can be expressed as

\displaystyle T_{{{\cal B}\overline{\cal D}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle i\frac{G_{F}}{\sqrt{2}}V_{ub}\bar{l}_{L}\gamma_{\mu}\nu_{L}

(25)

\displaystyle\times\bar{u}(p_{{\cal D}},\lambda_{\cal B})\Big\{\Big[g^{\prime}_{1}p_{{\cal B}\nu}\gamma_{\mu}+ig^{\prime}_{2}\sigma_{\mu\rho}p_{{\cal B}\nu}q^{\rho}+g^{\prime}_{3}p_{{\cal B}\nu}q_{\mu}+g^{\prime}_{4}p_{{\cal B}\nu}p_{{\cal B}\mu}+g^{\prime}_{5}g_{\nu\mu}+g^{\prime}_{6}q_{\nu}\gamma_{\mu}

\displaystyle\quad+ig^{\prime}_{7}\sigma_{\mu\rho}q_{\nu}q^{\rho}+g^{\prime}_{8}q_{\nu}q_{\mu}+g^{\prime}_{9}q_{\nu}p_{{\cal B}\mu}\Big]\gamma_{5}-\Big[f^{\prime}_{1}p_{{\cal B}\nu}\gamma_{\mu}+if^{\prime}_{2}\sigma_{\mu\rho}p_{{\cal B}\nu}q^{\rho}+f^{\prime}_{3}p_{{\cal B}\nu}q_{\mu}

\displaystyle\quad+f^{\prime}_{4}p_{{\cal B}\nu}p_{{\cal B}\mu}+f^{\prime}_{5}g_{\nu\mu}+f^{\prime}_{6}q_{\nu}\gamma_{\mu}+if^{\prime}_{7}\sigma_{\mu\rho}q_{\nu}q^{\rho}+f^{\prime}_{8}q_{\nu}q_{\mu}+f^{\prime}_{9}q_{\nu}p_{{\cal B}\mu}\Big]\Big\}v^{\nu}(p_{\overline{\cal D}},\lambda_{\overline{\cal D}}),

and

\displaystyle T_{{{\cal D}\overline{\cal B}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle i\frac{G_{F}}{\sqrt{2}}V_{ub}\bar{l}_{L}\gamma_{\mu}\nu_{L}

(26)

\displaystyle\times\bar{u}^{\nu}(p_{{\cal D}},\lambda_{\cal D})\Big\{\Big[g^{\prime\prime}_{1}p_{\overline{\cal B}\nu}\gamma_{\mu}+ig^{\prime\prime}_{2}\sigma_{\mu\rho}p_{\overline{\cal B}\nu}q^{\rho}+g^{\prime\prime}_{3}p_{\overline{\cal B}\nu}q_{\mu}+g^{\prime\prime}_{4}p_{\overline{\cal B}\nu}p_{\overline{\cal B}\mu}+g^{\prime\prime}_{5}g_{\nu\mu}+g^{\prime\prime}_{6}q_{\nu}\gamma_{\mu}

\displaystyle\quad+ig^{\prime\prime}_{7}\sigma_{\mu\rho}q_{\nu}q^{\rho}+g^{\prime\prime}_{8}q_{\nu}q_{\mu}+g^{\prime\prime}_{9}q_{\nu}p_{\overline{\cal B}\mu}\Big]\gamma_{5}-\Big[f^{\prime\prime}_{1}p_{\overline{\cal B}\nu}\gamma_{\mu}+if^{\prime\prime}_{2}\sigma_{\mu\rho}p_{\overline{\cal B}\nu}q^{\rho}+f^{\prime\prime}_{3}p_{\overline{\cal B}\nu}q_{\mu}

\displaystyle\quad+f^{\prime\prime}_{4}p_{\overline{\cal B}\nu}p_{\overline{\cal B}\mu}+f^{\prime\prime}_{5}g_{\nu\mu}+f^{\prime\prime}_{6}q_{\nu}\gamma_{\mu}+if^{\prime\prime}_{7}\sigma_{\mu\rho}q_{\nu}q^{\rho}+f^{\prime\prime}_{8}q_{\nu}q_{\mu}+f^{\prime\prime}_{9}q_{\nu}p_{\overline{\cal B}\mu}\Big]\Big\}v(p_{\overline{\cal B}},\lambda_{\overline{\cal B}}),

where
u^{\mu},\,v^{\mu}
are the Rarita-Schwinger vector spinors.
Finally the tree topological amplitude for
\overline{B}_{q}\to{{\cal D}\overline{\cal D}}^{\prime}l\bar{\nu}
decay is given by

\displaystyle T_{{{\cal D}\overline{\cal D}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle i\frac{G_{F}}{\sqrt{2}}V_{ub}\bar{l}_{L}\gamma_{\mu}\nu_{L}

(27)

\displaystyle\times\bar{u}_{\nu}(p_{{\cal D}},\lambda_{\cal D})\Big\{\Big[g^{\prime\prime\prime}_{1}\gamma_{\mu}+ig^{\prime\prime\prime}_{2}\sigma_{\mu\rho}q^{\rho}+g^{\prime\prime\prime}_{3}q_{\mu}+g^{\prime\prime\prime}_{4}(p_{{\cal D}}+p_{\overline{\cal D}^{\prime}})_{\mu}+g^{\prime\prime\prime}_{5}(p_{{\cal D}}-p_{\overline{\cal D}^{\prime}})_{\mu}]\gamma_{5}

\displaystyle-[f^{\prime\prime\prime}_{1}\gamma_{\mu}+if^{\prime\prime\prime}_{2}\sigma_{\mu\nu}q^{\nu}+f^{\prime\prime\prime}_{3}q_{\mu}+f^{\prime\prime\prime}_{4}(p_{{\cal D}}+p_{\overline{\cal D}^{\prime}})_{\mu}+f^{\prime\prime\prime}_{5}(p_{{\cal D}}-p_{\overline{\cal D}^{\prime}})_{\mu}]\}\Big]\Big\}v^{\nu}(p_{\overline{\cal D}},\lambda_{\overline{\cal D}})

\displaystyle+\dots,

where terms such as

\bar{u}_{\nu}p_{\overline{\cal D}}^{\nu}\{\dots\}p_{\cal D\sigma}u^{\sigma}
,

\bar{u}_{\nu}q^{\nu}\{\dots\}p_{\cal D\sigma}u^{\sigma}
,

\bar{u}_{\nu}p_{\overline{\cal D}}^{\nu}\{\dots\}q_{\sigma}u^{\sigma}
,

\bar{u}_{\nu}q^{\nu}\{\dots\}q_{\sigma}u^{\sigma}

are not shown explicitly in the above equation.
The annihilation amplitude
A_{{\cal D}\overline{\cal D}}
can be expressed similarly.
Topological amplitudes for loop induced
\overline{B}_{q}\to{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}^{\prime}\nu\bar{\nu}
decays can be obtained using the above equations and Eq. (
23
).

The topological amplitudes for
\overline{B}_{q}\to{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}^{\prime}l\bar{\nu}
decays are given in Eq. (
24
).
Fo illustration
we follow refs
Geng:2021sdl
;
Hsiao:2022uzx
to use

\displaystyle g^{(i)}_{j}=\frac{G^{(i)}_{j}}{t^{3}},\quad f^{(i)}_{j}=\frac{F^{(i)}_{f_{j}}}{t^{3}},\quad g^{(a)}_{j}=f^{(a)}_{j}=0,

(28)

where
G^{(i)}_{j}
and
F^{(i)}_{j}
are some constants to be specified later,
t\equiv m^{2}_{{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}^{\prime}}
and the last equation corresponds to the
A_{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}=PBA_{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}=0
case.
The values of the constants
G^{(i)}_{j}
and
F^{(i)}_{j}
are extracted from refs
Geng:2021sdl
;
Hsiao:2022uzx

but slightly modified to match the asymptotic relations in Appendix A,
where it is known that there are asymptotic relations
Brodsky:1980sx
in the matrix elements of octet and decuplet baryons in the large momentum transfer region,
and to match the
B^{-}\to p\bar{p}l\bar{\nu}
data.
In fact we find that the corresponding
F^{(i)}_{3,4,5}
used in ref.
Geng:2021sdl
do not satisfy the correct asymptotic relations, which can however be satisfied by adding a minus sign to their
F^{(i)}_{3,4,5}
.
Nevertheless as we shall see that the modification do not significantly affect the
B^{-}\to{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}^{\prime}l\bar{\nu}
rates.

The values of
G^{(i)}_{j}
and
F^{(i)}_{j}
are shown in Table
2
.
Explicitly we use

G^{(i)}_{1}=\eta_{1}m_{B}(e^{(i)}_{\parallel}C_{LL}-e^{(i)}_{\overline{\parallel}}C_{RR})/3
,

F^{(i)}_{1}=\eta_{1}m_{B}(e^{(i)}_{\parallel}C_{LL}+e^{(i)}_{\overline{\parallel}}C_{RR})/3
,

G^{(i)}_{2,3,4,5}=-F^{(i)}_{2,3,4,5}=-\eta_{1}\times 2e^{(i)}_{F}C_{LR}/3
with
(C_{LL},C_{RR},C_{LR})=(17.78,-11.67,6.41)
GeV
4

Geng:2021sdl
for Model 1,
and

G^{(i)}_{1}=\eta_{2}m_{B}(e^{(i)}_{\parallel}D_{\parallel}-e^{(i)}_{\overline{\parallel}}D_{\overline{\parallel}})/3
,

F^{(i)}_{1}=\eta_{2}m_{B}(e^{(i)}_{\parallel}D_{\parallel}+e^{(i)}_{\overline{\parallel}}D_{\overline{\parallel}})/3
,

G^{(i)}_{2,3,4,5}=-F^{(i)}_{2,3,4,5}=-\eta_{2}\times 2e^{(i)}_{F}D_{2,3,4,5}/3

with
(D_{\parallel},D_{\overline{\parallel}})=(11.2,323.3)
GeV
5
and
D_{2,3,4,5}=(47.7,442.2,-38.7,-80.7)
GeV
4

Hsiao:2022uzx
for Model 2,
where the factors
\eta_{1}=0.93
and
\eta_{2}=0.75
are introduced to match the central value of the
B^{-}\to p\bar{p}l\bar{\nu}
data and
e^{(i)}_{\parallel,\overline{\parallel},F}
are given in
Eq. (
93
).
Note that the sign of
D_{5}
is flipped from the one from ref.
Hsiao:2022uzx
, to match the definitions of form factors
f^{(i)}_{5}
and
g^{(i)}_{5}
in Eq. (
24
).

The topological amplitudes of
\overline{B}_{q}\to{{\cal B}\overline{\cal D}}l\bar{\nu}
and
\overline{B}_{q}\to{{\cal D}\overline{\cal B}}l\bar{\nu}
decays are given in Eqs. (
25
) and (
26
).
For simplicity, we only concentrate on the contributions from
g^{\prime}_{1,2,3,4,5}
,
f^{\prime}_{1,2,3,4,5}
,
g^{\prime\prime}_{1,2,3,4,5}
and
f^{\prime\prime}_{1,2,3,4,5}
,
by assuming that their contributions are dominant.
This working assumption can be checked or relaxed when data of
\overline{B}_{q}\to{{\cal B}\overline{\cal D}}l\bar{\nu}
and
\overline{B}_{q}\to{{\cal D}\overline{\cal B}}l\bar{\nu}
decays become available.

It is known that in the asymptotic limit form factors of octet-octet and octet decuplet are related
Brodsky:1980sx
.
As shown in Appendix
A
in the asymptotic limit
T_{{{\cal B}\overline{\cal D}}}
,
T_{{{\cal B}\overline{\cal D}}}
and
T_{i{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}
are related and have similar structure.
These impose constrains on the form factors.
For simplicity we assume that these form factors have similar forms as the form factors in Eq. (
28
).
Using Eqs. (
28
), (
) and (
93
), we have

\displaystyle g^{\prime}_{1,2,3,4}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{m_{\overline{\cal D}}G^{\prime}_{1,2,3,4}}{t^{4}},\,\,g^{\prime}_{5}=\frac{m_{\overline{\cal D}}G^{\prime}_{5}}{t^{3}},\quad f^{\prime}_{1,2,3,4}=\frac{m_{\overline{\cal D}}F^{\prime}_{1,2,3,4}}{t^{4}},\,\,f^{\prime}_{5}=\frac{m_{\overline{\cal D}}F^{\prime}_{5}}{t^{3}},

\displaystyle g^{\prime\prime}_{1,2,3,4}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{m_{\cal D}G^{\prime\prime}_{1,2,3,4}}{t^{4}},\,\,g^{\prime\prime}_{5}=\frac{m_{\cal D}G^{\prime\prime}_{5}}{t^{3}},\quad f^{\prime\prime}_{1,2,3,4}=\frac{m_{\cal D}F_{1,2,3,4}}{t^{4}},\,\,f^{\prime\prime}_{5}=\frac{m_{\cal D}F^{\prime\prime}_{5}}{t^{3}},

(29)

with

\displaystyle G^{\prime}_{1,2,3}=-\sqrt{6}G^{(i)}_{1,2,3},\quad G^{\prime}_{4}=-\sqrt{6}(G^{(i)}_{4}+G^{(i)}_{5}),\quad G^{\prime}_{5}=\sqrt{\frac{3}{2}}(G^{(i)}_{4}-G^{(i)}_{5}),

\displaystyle F^{\prime}_{1,2,3}=-\sqrt{6}F^{(i)}_{1,2,3},\quad F^{\prime}_{4}=-\sqrt{6}(F^{(i)}_{4}+F^{(i)}_{5}),\quad F^{\prime}_{5}=\sqrt{\frac{3}{2}}(F^{(i)}_{4}-F^{(i)}_{5}),

(30)

but with
(e^{(i)}_{\parallel},e^{(i)}_{\overline{\parallel}},e^{(i)}_{F})
in
G^{(i)}_{j},F^{(i)}_{j}
replaced by
(e^{\prime}_{\parallel},e^{\prime}_{\overline{\parallel}},e^{\prime}_{F})
,
and

\displaystyle G^{\prime\prime}_{1,2,3}=-\sqrt{6}G^{(i)}_{1,2,3},\quad G^{\prime\prime}_{4}=\sqrt{6}(G^{(i)}_{5}-G^{(i)}_{4}),\quad G^{\prime\prime}_{5}=\sqrt{\frac{3}{2}}(G^{(i)}_{4}+G^{(i)}_{5}),

\displaystyle F^{\prime\prime}_{1,2,3}=-\sqrt{6}F^{(i)}_{1,2,3},\quad F^{\prime\prime}_{4}=\sqrt{6}(F^{(i)}_{5}-F^{(i)}_{4}),\quad F^{\prime\prime}_{5}=\sqrt{\frac{3}{2}}(F^{(i)}_{4}+F^{(i)}_{5}),

(31)

but with
(e^{(i)}_{\parallel},e^{(i)}_{\overline{\parallel}},e^{(i)}_{F})
in
G^{(i)}_{j},F^{(i)}_{j}
replaced by
(e^{\prime\prime}_{\parallel},e^{\prime\prime}_{\overline{\parallel}},e^{\prime\prime}_{F})
.
Note that the above constants are related in the asymptotic limit and, consequently, inputs from Model 1 and 2 have been used in the above relations.
The values of these constants in Model 1 and 2 are given in Table
3
.

In the model calculations of
\overline{B}_{q}\to{{\cal D}\overline{\cal D}}^{\prime}l\nu
and
{{\cal D}\overline{\cal D}}^{\prime}\nu\bar{\nu}
decay rates,
we use Eq. (
27
) for the tree topological amplitude,
where we neglect terms, such as

\bar{u}_{\nu}p_{\overline{\cal D}}^{\nu}\{\dots\}p_{\cal D\sigma}u^{\sigma}
,

\bar{u}_{\nu}q^{\nu}\{\dots\}p_{\cal D\sigma}u^{\sigma}
,

\bar{u}_{\nu}p_{\overline{\cal D}}^{\nu}\{\dots\}q_{\sigma}u^{\sigma}
,

\bar{u}_{\nu}q^{\nu}\{\dots\}q_{\sigma}u^{\sigma}
,
for simplicity.
This working assumption can be checked or modified once data is available.
As in
\overline{B}_{q}\to{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}^{\prime}l\bar{\nu}
decays, we neglect the contribution from the annihilation topological amplitude,
A_{{\cal D}\overline{\cal D}}
.
Using Eqs. (
28
), (
93
) and (
98
), the form factors are given by

\displaystyle g^{\prime\prime\prime}_{j}=m_{\cal D}m_{\overline{\cal D}}\frac{G^{\prime\prime\prime}_{j}}{t^{4}},\quad f^{\prime\prime\prime}_{j}=m_{\cal D}m_{\overline{\cal D}}\frac{F^{\prime\prime\prime}_{j}}{t^{4}},

(32)

with

\displaystyle G^{\prime\prime\prime}_{j}=-3G^{(i)}_{j},\quad F^{\prime\prime\prime}_{j}=-3F^{(i)}_{j},

(33)

but with
(e^{(i)}_{\parallel},e^{(i)}_{\overline{\parallel}},e^{(i)}_{F})
in
G^{(i)}_{j},F^{(i)}_{j}
replaced by
(e^{\prime\prime\prime}_{\parallel},e^{\prime\prime\prime}_{\overline{\parallel}},e^{\prime\prime\prime}_{F})
.
Note that in the asymptotic limit the above form factors are related to those in
\overline{B}_{q}\to{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}^{\prime}l\bar{\nu}
decays via Eq. (
93
), and, consequently, inputs from Model 1 and 2 have been used.
The values of these constants in Model 1 and 2 are given in Table
4
.

III
Results on amplitudes

III.1
Decay amplitudes in terms of topological amplitudes

Using the above Hamiltonian the decompositions of amplitudes for
\overline{B}_{q}\to{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}^{\prime}l\bar{\nu}
,
{{\cal B}\overline{\cal D}}l\bar{\nu}
,
{{\cal D}\overline{\cal B}}l\bar{\nu}
,
{{\cal D}\overline{\cal D}}^{\prime}l\bar{\nu}
and

\overline{B}_{q}\to{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}^{\prime}\nu\bar{\nu}
,
{{\cal B}\overline{\cal D}}\nu\bar{\nu}
,
{{\cal D}\overline{\cal B}}\nu\bar{\nu}
,
{{\cal D}\overline{\cal D}}^{\prime}\nu\bar{\nu}
decays are shown in Tables

5
,
6
,
7
and
8
.
These tables are some of the main results of this work.

As shown in Table
5
we have three topological amplitudes,
T_{2{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}
,
T_{1{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}
and
A_{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}
, in
\overline{B}_{q}\to{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}^{\prime}l\bar{\nu}
decays,
and three topological amplitudes,
PB_{2{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}
,
PB_{1{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}
and
PBA_{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}
, in
\overline{B}_{q}\to{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}^{\prime}\nu\bar{\nu}
decays.
As shown in Table
6
we need one topological amplitude,
T_{{{\cal B}\overline{\cal D}}}
, in
\overline{B}_{q}\to{{\cal B}\overline{\cal D}}l\bar{\nu}
decays,
and
one topological amplitude,
PB_{{{\cal B}\overline{\cal D}}}
, in
\overline{B}_{q}\to{{\cal B}\overline{\cal D}}\nu\bar{\nu}
decays.
Similarly, as shown in Table
7
we have
one topological amplitude,
T_{{{\cal D}\overline{\cal B}}}
, in
\overline{B}_{q}\to{{\cal D}\overline{\cal B}}l\bar{\nu}
decays,
and one topological amplitude,
PB_{{{\cal D}\overline{\cal B}}}
, in
\overline{B}_{q}\to{{\cal D}\overline{\cal B}}\nu\bar{\nu}
decays.
Finally as shown in Table
6
we have
two topological amplitude,
T_{{{\cal D}\overline{\cal D}}}
and
A_{{\cal D}\overline{\cal D}}
, in
\overline{B}_{q}\to{{\cal D}\overline{\cal D}}^{\prime}l\bar{\nu}
decays,
and two topological amplitudes,
PB_{{{\cal D}\overline{\cal D}}}
and
PBA_{{\cal D}\overline{\cal D}}
, in
\overline{B}_{q}\to{{\cal D}\overline{\cal D}}^{\prime}\nu\bar{\nu}
decays.

As the numbers of independent topological amplitudes are highly limited comparing to the numbers of the decay modes,
there are plenty of relations on
\overline{B}_{q}\to{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}^{\prime}l\bar{\nu}
and
{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}^{\prime}\nu\bar{\nu}
decay amplitudes.
These relations will be given in the following discussion.

III.2
Relations of decay amplitudes

As noted previously since the number of topological amplitudes are quite limited, relations of decay amplitudes are expected.
The following relations are obtained by using the decomposition of amplitudes shown in
Tables
5
,
6
,
7
and
8
.

In
\overline{B}_{q}\to{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}^{\prime}l\bar{\nu}
decays, we have the following relations on amplitudes,

\displaystyle A(\overline{B}{}^{0}\to p\bar{n}l\bar{\nu})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle A(\overline{B}{}^{0}_{s}\to\Sigma^{+}\overline{\Xi^{0}}l\bar{\nu})=\sqrt{2}A(\overline{B}{}^{0}_{s}\to\Sigma^{0}\overline{\Xi^{-}}l\bar{\nu}),

(34)

\displaystyle A(B^{-}\to n\bar{n}l\bar{\nu})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle A(B^{-}\to\Xi^{0}\overline{\Xi^{0}}l\bar{\nu}),

(35)

\displaystyle A(\overline{B}{}^{0}\to\Xi^{0}\overline{\Xi^{-}}l\bar{\nu})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sqrt{2}A(\overline{B}{}^{0}_{s}\to p\overline{\Sigma^{0}}l\bar{\nu})=A(\overline{B}{}^{0}_{s}\to n\overline{\Sigma^{-}}l\bar{\nu}),

(36)

\displaystyle A(B^{-}\to p\bar{p}l\bar{\nu})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle A(B^{-}\to\Sigma^{+}\overline{\Sigma^{+}}l\bar{\nu}),

(37)

\displaystyle A(\overline{B}{}^{0}\to\Sigma^{+}\overline{\Sigma^{0}}l\bar{\nu})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-A(\overline{B}{}^{0}\to\Sigma^{0}\overline{\Sigma^{-}}l\bar{\nu})=\sqrt{3}A(\overline{B}{}^{0}_{s}\to p\overline{\Lambda}l\bar{\nu}),

(38)

\displaystyle\sqrt{2}A(B^{-}\to\Sigma^{0}\overline{\Lambda}l\bar{\nu})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sqrt{2}A(B^{-}\to\Lambda\overline{\Sigma^{0}}l\bar{\nu})=A(\overline{B}{}^{0}\to\Sigma^{+}\overline{\Lambda}l\bar{\nu})=A(\overline{B}{}^{0}\to\Lambda\overline{\Sigma^{-}}l\bar{\nu}),

(39)

\displaystyle A(B^{-}\to\Sigma^{-}\overline{\Sigma^{-}}l\bar{\nu})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle A(B^{-}\to\Xi^{-}\overline{\Xi^{-}}l\bar{\nu}),

(40)

and

\displaystyle A(B^{-}\to p\bar{p}l\bar{\nu})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle A(\overline{B}{}^{0}\to p\bar{n}l\bar{\nu})+A(B^{-}\to n\bar{n}l\bar{\nu})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2A(B^{-}\to\Sigma^{0}\overline{\Sigma^{0}}l\bar{\nu})+A(B^{-}\to\Sigma^{-}\overline{\Sigma^{-}}l\bar{\nu}),

\displaystyle 2\sqrt{3}A(B^{-}\to\Sigma^{0}\overline{\Lambda}l\bar{\nu})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle A(\overline{B}{}^{0}\to p\bar{n}l\bar{\nu})+A(\overline{B}{}^{0}\to\Xi^{0}\overline{\Xi^{-}}l\bar{\nu}),

\displaystyle\sqrt{6}A(B^{-}\to\Lambda\overline{\Lambda}l\bar{\nu})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle A(\overline{B}{}^{0}\to\Sigma^{+}\overline{\Lambda}l\bar{\nu})+\sqrt{6}A(B^{-}\to n\bar{n}l\bar{\nu}),

\displaystyle-\sqrt{6}A(\overline{B}{}^{0}_{s}\to p\overline{\Lambda}l\bar{\nu})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2A(\overline{B}{}^{0}_{s}\to\Sigma^{+}\overline{\Xi^{0}}l\bar{\nu})-A(\overline{B}{}^{0}_{s}\to n\overline{\Sigma^{-}}l\bar{\nu}),

\displaystyle\sqrt{6}A(\overline{B}{}^{0}_{s}\to\Lambda\overline{\Xi^{-}}l\bar{\nu})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle A(\overline{B}{}^{0}_{s}\to\Sigma^{+}\overline{\Xi^{0}}l\bar{\nu})-2A(\overline{B}{}^{0}_{s}\to n\overline{\Sigma^{-}}l\bar{\nu}).

(41)

Similarly, for
\overline{B}_{q}\to{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}^{\prime}\nu\bar{\nu}
decays, we have

\displaystyle A(B^{-}\to\Xi^{0}\overline{\Sigma^{+}}\nu\bar{\nu})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sqrt{2}A(B^{-}\to\Xi^{-}\overline{\Sigma^{0}}\nu\bar{\nu})=-\sqrt{2}A(\overline{B}{}^{0}\to\Xi^{0}\overline{\Sigma^{0}}\nu\bar{\nu})

(42)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle A(\overline{B}{}^{0}\to\Xi^{-}\overline{\Sigma^{-}}\nu\bar{\nu}),

\displaystyle-\sqrt{2}A(B^{-}\to\Sigma^{0}\bar{p}\nu\bar{\nu})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-A(B^{-}\to\Sigma^{-}\bar{n}\nu\bar{\nu})=-A(\overline{B}{}^{0}\to\Sigma^{+}\bar{p}\nu\bar{\nu})

(43)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sqrt{2}A(\overline{B}{}^{0}\to\Sigma^{0}\bar{n}\nu\bar{\nu}),

\displaystyle A(\overline{B}{}^{0}_{s}\to\Sigma^{+}\overline{\Sigma^{+}}\nu\bar{\nu})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle A(\overline{B}{}^{0}_{s}\to\Sigma^{0}\overline{\Sigma^{0}}\nu\bar{\nu})=A(\overline{B}{}^{0}_{s}\to\Sigma^{-}\overline{\Sigma^{-}}\nu\bar{\nu}),

(44)

\displaystyle A(\overline{B}{}^{0}_{s}\to\Xi^{0}\overline{\Xi^{0}}\nu\bar{\nu})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle A(\overline{B}{}^{0}_{s}\to\Xi^{-}\overline{\Xi^{-}}\nu\bar{\nu}),

(45)

\displaystyle A(B^{-}\to\Lambda\bar{p}\nu\bar{\nu})=A(\overline{B}{}^{0}\to\Lambda\bar{n}\nu\bar{\nu}),

(46)

\displaystyle A(\overline{B}{}^{0}_{s}\to p\bar{p}\nu\bar{\nu})=A(\overline{B}{}^{0}_{s}\to n\bar{n}\nu\bar{\nu}),

(47)

and

\displaystyle\sqrt{6}A(\overline{B}{}^{0}\to\Xi^{0}\overline{\Lambda}\nu\bar{\nu})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle A(\overline{B}{}^{0}\to\Xi^{-}\overline{\Sigma^{-}}\nu\bar{\nu})-A(\overline{B}{}^{0}\to\Sigma^{+}\bar{p}\nu\bar{\nu}),

\displaystyle\sqrt{6}A(B^{-}\to\Xi^{-}\overline{\Lambda}\nu\bar{\nu})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle A(B^{-}\to\Xi^{0}\overline{\Sigma^{+}}\nu\bar{\nu})-2A(B^{-}\to\Sigma^{-}\bar{n}\nu\bar{\nu}),

\displaystyle-\sqrt{6}A(B^{-}\to\Lambda\bar{p}\nu\bar{\nu})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2A(B^{-}\to\Xi^{0}\overline{\Sigma^{+}}\nu\bar{\nu})-A(B^{-}\to\Sigma^{-}\bar{n}\nu\bar{\nu}),

\displaystyle\sqrt{3}A(\overline{B}{}^{0}_{s}\to\Lambda\overline{\Lambda}\nu\bar{\nu})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\sqrt{2}A(B^{-}\to\Lambda\bar{p}\nu\bar{\nu})+\sqrt{3}A(\overline{B}{}^{0}_{s}\to p\bar{p}\nu\bar{\nu}).

(48)

For
\overline{B}_{q}\to{{\cal B}\overline{\cal D}}l\bar{\nu}
decays, there is only one topological amplitude, namely
T_{{\cal B}\overline{\cal D}}
. Therefore, all decay amplitudes are related,

\displaystyle-A(B^{-}\to p\overline{\Delta^{+}}l\bar{\nu})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-A(B^{-}\to n\overline{\Delta^{0}}l\bar{\nu})=A(B^{-}\to\Sigma^{+}\overline{\Sigma^{*+}}l\bar{\nu})=2A(B^{-}\to\Sigma^{0}\overline{\Sigma^{*0}}l\bar{\nu})

(49)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle A(B^{-}\to\Xi^{0}\overline{\Xi^{*0}}l\bar{\nu})=\frac{2}{\sqrt{3}}A(B^{-}\to\Lambda\overline{\Sigma^{*0}}l\bar{\nu})=-A(\overline{B}{}^{0}\to p\overline{\Delta^{0}}l\bar{\nu})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{\sqrt{3}}A(\overline{B}{}^{0}\to n\overline{\Delta^{-}}l\bar{\nu})=\sqrt{2}A(\overline{B}{}^{0}\to\Sigma^{+}\overline{\Sigma^{*0}}l\bar{\nu})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\sqrt{2}A(\overline{B}{}^{0}\to\Sigma^{0}\overline{\Sigma^{*-}}l\bar{\nu})=A(\overline{B}{}^{0}\to\Xi^{0}\overline{\Xi^{*-}}l\bar{\nu})=\sqrt{\frac{2}{3}}A(\overline{B}{}^{0}\to\Lambda\overline{\Sigma^{*-}}l\bar{\nu})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\sqrt{2}A(\overline{B}{}^{0}_{s}\to p\overline{\Sigma^{*0}}l\bar{\nu})=-A(\overline{B}{}^{0}_{s}\to n\overline{\Sigma^{*-}}l\bar{\nu})=A(\overline{B}{}^{0}_{s}\to\Sigma^{+}\overline{\Xi^{*0}}l\bar{\nu})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\sqrt{2}A(\overline{B}{}^{0}_{s}\to\Sigma^{0}\overline{\Xi^{*-}}l\bar{\nu})=\frac{1}{\sqrt{3}}A(\overline{B}{}^{0}_{s}\to\Xi^{0}\overline{\Omega^{-}}l\bar{\nu})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{2}{3}}A(\overline{B}{}^{0}_{s}\to\Lambda\overline{\Xi^{*-}}l\bar{\nu}).

Similarly, for
\overline{B}_{q}\to{{\cal B}\overline{\cal D}}\nu\bar{\nu}
decays, there is only one topological amplitude, namely
PB_{{\cal B}\overline{\cal D}}
. Hence, all decay amplitudes are related.
Explicitly, we have the following relations,

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{\sqrt{6}}A(B^{-}\to\Sigma^{+}\overline{\Delta^{++}}\nu\bar{\nu})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}A(B^{-}\to\Sigma^{0}\overline{\Delta^{+}}\nu\bar{\nu})=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}A(B^{-}\to\Sigma^{-}\overline{\Delta^{0}}\nu\bar{\nu})

(50)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}A(B^{-}\to\Xi^{0}\overline{\Sigma^{*+}}\nu\bar{\nu})=A(B^{-}\to\Xi^{-}\overline{\Sigma^{*0}}\nu\bar{\nu})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}A(\overline{B}{}^{0}\to\Sigma^{+}\overline{\Delta^{+}}\nu\bar{\nu})=\frac{1}{2}A(\overline{B}{}^{0}\to\Sigma^{0}\overline{\Delta^{0}}\nu\bar{\nu})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{6}}A(\overline{B}{}^{0}\to\Sigma^{-}\overline{\Delta^{-}}\nu\bar{\nu})=-A(\overline{B}{}^{0}\to\Xi^{0}\overline{\Sigma^{*0}}\nu\bar{\nu})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}A(\overline{B}{}^{0}\to\Xi^{-}\overline{\Sigma^{*-}}\nu\bar{\nu})=-\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}A(\overline{B}{}^{0}_{s}\to\Sigma^{+}\overline{\Sigma^{*+}}\nu\bar{\nu})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}A(\overline{B}{}^{0}_{s}\to\Sigma^{0}\overline{\Sigma^{*0}}\nu\bar{\nu})=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}A(\overline{B}{}^{0}_{s}\to\Sigma^{-}\overline{\Sigma^{*-}}\nu\bar{\nu})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}A(\overline{B}{}^{0}_{s}\to\Xi^{0}\overline{\Xi^{*0}}\nu\bar{\nu})=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}A(\overline{B}{}^{0}_{s}\to\Xi^{-}\overline{\Xi^{*-}}\nu\bar{\nu}).

For
\overline{B}_{q}\to{{\cal D}\overline{\cal B}}l\bar{\nu}
decays, there is only one topological amplitude
(T_{{\cal D}\overline{\cal B}})
, while for
\overline{B}_{q}\to{{\cal D}\overline{\cal B}}\nu\bar{\nu}
decays, there is also only one topological amplitude (
PB_{{\cal D}\overline{\cal B}}
). Hence, the decay amplitudes are highly related and we have the following relations for
\overline{B}_{q}\to{{\cal D}\overline{\cal B}}l\bar{\nu}
decays,

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}A(B^{-}\to\Delta^{+}\bar{p}l\bar{\nu})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}A(B^{-}\to\Delta^{0}\bar{n}l\bar{\nu})=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}A(B^{-}\to\Sigma^{*+}\overline{\Sigma^{+}}l\bar{\nu})

(51)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\sqrt{2}A(B^{-}\to\Sigma^{*0}\overline{\Sigma^{0}}l\bar{\nu})=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}A(B^{-}\to\Xi^{*0}\overline{\Xi^{0}}l\bar{\nu})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{2}{3}}A(B^{-}\to\Sigma^{*0}\overline{\Lambda}l\bar{\nu})=\frac{1}{\sqrt{6}}A(\overline{B}{}^{0}\to\Delta^{++}\bar{p}l\bar{\nu})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}A(\overline{B}{}^{0}\to\Delta^{+}\bar{n}l\bar{\nu})=-A(\overline{B}{}^{0}\to\Sigma^{*+}\overline{\Sigma^{0}}l\bar{\nu})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-A(\overline{B}{}^{0}\to\Sigma^{*0}\overline{\Sigma^{-}}l\bar{\nu})=-\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}A(\overline{B}{}^{0}\to\Xi^{*0}\overline{\Xi^{-}}l\bar{\nu})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{3}}A(\overline{B}{}^{0}\to\Sigma^{*+}\overline{\Lambda}l\bar{\nu})=-\frac{1}{\sqrt{6}}A(\overline{B}{}^{0}_{s}\to\Delta^{++}\overline{\Sigma^{+}}l\bar{\nu})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}A(\overline{B}{}^{0}_{s}\to\Delta^{+}\overline{\Sigma^{0}}l\bar{\nu})=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}A(\overline{B}{}^{0}_{s}\to\Delta^{0}\overline{\Sigma^{-}}l\bar{\nu})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}A(\overline{B}{}^{0}_{s}\to\Sigma^{*+}\overline{\Xi^{0}}l\bar{\nu})=A(\overline{B}{}^{0}_{s}\to\Sigma^{*0}\overline{\Xi^{-}}l\bar{\nu}),

and

\displaystyle-A(B^{-}\to\Sigma^{*0}\bar{p}\nu\bar{\nu})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}A(B^{-}\to\Sigma^{*-}\bar{n}\nu\bar{\nu})=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}A(B^{-}\to\Xi^{*0}\overline{\Sigma^{+}}\nu\bar{\nu})

(52)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-A(B^{-}\to\Xi^{*-}\overline{\Sigma^{0}}\nu\bar{\nu})=\frac{1}{\sqrt{6}}A(B^{-}\to\Omega^{-}\overline{\Xi^{0}}\nu\bar{\nu})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{3}}A(B^{-}\to\Xi^{*-}\overline{\Lambda}\nu\bar{\nu})=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}A(\overline{B}{}^{0}\to\Sigma^{*+}\bar{p}\nu\bar{\nu})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle A(\overline{B}{}^{0}\to\Sigma^{*0}\bar{n}\nu\bar{\nu})=-A(\overline{B}{}^{0}\to\Xi^{*0}\overline{\Sigma^{0}}\nu\bar{\nu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}A(\overline{B}{}^{0}\to\Xi^{*-}\overline{\Sigma^{-}}\nu\bar{\nu})=-\frac{1}{\sqrt{6}}A(\overline{B}{}^{0}\to\Omega^{-}\overline{\Xi^{-}}\nu\bar{\nu})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{\sqrt{3}}A(\overline{B}{}^{0}\to\Xi^{*0}\overline{\Lambda}\nu\bar{\nu})=-\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}A(\overline{B}{}^{0}_{s}\to\Sigma^{*+}\overline{\Sigma^{+}}\nu\bar{\nu})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}A(\overline{B}{}^{0}_{s}\to\Sigma^{*0}\overline{\Sigma^{0}}\nu\bar{\nu})=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}A(\overline{B}{}^{0}_{s}\to\Sigma^{*-}\overline{\Sigma^{-}}\nu\bar{\nu})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}A(\overline{B}{}^{0}_{s}\to\Xi^{*0}\overline{\Xi^{0}}\nu\bar{\nu})=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}A(\overline{B}{}^{0}_{s}\to\Xi^{*-}\overline{\Xi^{-}}\nu\bar{\nu}),

for
\overline{B}_{q}\to{{\cal D}\overline{\cal B}}\nu\bar{\nu}
decays.

For
\overline{B}_{q}\to{{\cal D}\overline{\cal D}}^{\prime}l\bar{\nu}
decays, we have two topological amplitudes, namely
T_{{\cal D}\overline{\cal D}}
and
A_{{\cal D}\overline{\cal D}}
.
The decay amplitudes are related as following,

\displaystyle\sqrt{3}A(\overline{B}{}^{0}\to\Delta^{++}\overline{\Delta^{+}}l\bar{\nu})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}A(\overline{B}{}^{0}\to\Delta^{+}\overline{\Delta^{0}}l\bar{\nu})=\sqrt{3}A(\overline{B}{}^{0}\to\Delta^{0}\overline{\Delta^{-}}l\bar{\nu})

(53)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}A(\overline{B}{}^{0}\to\Sigma^{*+}\overline{\Sigma^{*0}}l\bar{\nu})=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}A(\overline{B}{}^{0}\to\Sigma^{*0}\overline{\Sigma^{*-}}l\bar{\nu})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle A(\overline{B}{}^{0}\to\Xi^{*0}\overline{\Xi^{*-}}l\bar{\nu})=\frac{1}{\sqrt{3}}A(\overline{B}{}^{0}_{s}\to\Delta^{++}\overline{\Sigma^{*+}}l\bar{\nu})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}A(\overline{B}{}^{0}_{s}\to\Delta^{+}\overline{\Sigma^{*0}}l\bar{\nu})=A(\overline{B}{}^{0}_{s}\to\Delta^{0}\overline{\Sigma^{*-}}l\bar{\nu})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}A(\overline{B}{}^{0}_{s}\to\Sigma^{*+}\overline{\Xi^{0}}l\bar{\nu})=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}A(\overline{B}{}^{0}_{s}\to\Sigma^{*0}\overline{\Xi^{*-}}l\bar{\nu})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{3}}A(\overline{B}{}^{0}_{s}\to\Xi^{*0}\overline{\Omega^{-}}l\bar{\nu})=\frac{1}{2}A(B^{-}\to\Delta^{+}\overline{\Delta^{+}}l\bar{\nu}),

\displaystyle A(B^{-}\to\Delta^{0}\overline{\Delta^{0}}l\bar{\nu})=A(B^{-}\to\Sigma^{*0}\overline{\Sigma^{*0}}l\bar{\nu})=A(B^{-}\to\Xi^{*0}\overline{\Xi^{*0}}l\bar{\nu}),

(54)

\displaystyle A(B^{-}\to\Delta^{-}\overline{\Delta^{-}}l\bar{\nu})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle A(B^{-}\to\Sigma^{*-}\overline{\Sigma^{*-}}l\bar{\nu})=A(B^{-}\to\Xi^{*-}\overline{\Xi^{*-}}l\bar{\nu})

(55)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle A(B^{-}\to\Omega^{-}\overline{\Omega^{-}}l\bar{\nu}),

and

\displaystyle A(B^{-}\to\Delta^{++}\overline{\Delta^{++}}l\bar{\nu})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle A(B^{-}\to\Delta^{0}\overline{\Delta^{0}}l\bar{\nu})+A(B^{-}\to\Delta^{-}\overline{\Delta^{-}}l\bar{\nu}),

\displaystyle A(B^{-}\to\Sigma^{*+}\overline{\Sigma^{*+}}l\bar{\nu})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle A(B^{-}\to\Delta^{+}\overline{\Delta^{+}}l\bar{\nu})+A(B^{-}\to\Sigma^{*-}\overline{\Sigma^{*-}}l\bar{\nu}).

(56)

Finally, for
\overline{B}_{q}\to{{\cal D}\overline{\cal D}}^{\prime}\nu\bar{\nu}
decays, we have two topological amplitudes, namely
PB_{{\cal D}\overline{\cal D}}
and
PBA_{{\cal D}\overline{\cal D}}
, giving
the following relations on the amplitudes,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{3}}A(B^{-}\to\Sigma^{*+}\overline{\Delta^{++}}\nu\bar{\nu})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}A(B^{-}\to\Sigma^{*0}\overline{\Delta^{+}}\nu\bar{\nu})=A(B^{-}\to\Sigma^{*-}\overline{\Delta^{0}}\nu\bar{\nu})

(57)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}A(B^{-}\to\Xi^{*0}\overline{\Sigma^{*+}}\nu\bar{\nu})=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}A(B^{-}\to\Xi^{*-}\overline{\Sigma^{*0}}\nu\bar{\nu})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{3}}A(B^{-}\to\Omega^{-}\overline{\Xi^{*0}}\nu\bar{\nu})=A(\overline{B}{}^{0}\to\Sigma^{*+}\overline{\Delta^{+}}\nu\bar{\nu})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}A(\overline{B}{}^{0}\to\Sigma^{*0}\overline{\Delta^{0}}\nu\bar{\nu})=\frac{1}{\sqrt{3}}A(\overline{B}{}^{0}\to\Sigma^{*-}\overline{\Delta^{-}}\nu\bar{\nu})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}A(\overline{B}{}^{0}\to\Xi^{*0}\overline{\Sigma^{*0}}\nu\bar{\nu})=\frac{1}{2}A(\overline{B}{}^{0}\to\Xi^{*-}\overline{\Sigma^{*-}}\nu\bar{\nu})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{3}}A(\overline{B}{}^{0}\to\Omega^{-}\overline{\Xi^{*-}}\nu\bar{\nu}),

\displaystyle A(\overline{B}{}^{0}_{s}\to\Sigma^{*+}\overline{\Sigma^{*+}}\nu\bar{\nu})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle A(\overline{B}{}^{0}_{s}\to\Sigma^{*0}\overline{\Sigma^{*0}}\nu\bar{\nu})=A(\overline{B}{}^{0}_{s}\to\Sigma^{*-}\overline{\Sigma^{*-}}\nu\bar{\nu}),

\displaystyle A(\overline{B}{}^{0}_{s}\to\Xi^{*0}\overline{\Xi^{*0}}\nu\bar{\nu})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle A(\overline{B}{}^{0}_{s}\to\Xi^{*-}\overline{\Xi^{*-}}\nu\bar{\nu}),

(58)

\displaystyle A(\overline{B}{}^{0}_{s}\to\Sigma^{*+}\overline{\Sigma^{*+}}\nu\bar{\nu})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle A(B^{-}\to\Sigma^{*-}\overline{\Delta^{0}}\nu\bar{\nu})+A(\overline{B}{}^{0}_{s}\to\Delta^{0}\overline{\Delta^{0}}\nu\bar{\nu}),

\displaystyle A(\overline{B}{}^{0}_{s}\to\Xi^{*0}\overline{\Xi^{*0}}\nu\bar{\nu})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle A(\overline{B}{}^{0}\to\Xi^{*-}\overline{\Sigma^{*-}}\nu\bar{\nu})+A(\overline{B}{}^{0}_{s}\to\Delta^{-}\overline{\Delta^{-}}\nu\bar{\nu}),

\displaystyle A(\overline{B}{}^{0}_{s}\to\Omega^{-}\overline{\Omega^{-}}\nu\bar{\nu})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle A(\overline{B}{}^{0}\to\Xi^{*-}\overline{\Sigma^{*-}}\nu\bar{\nu})+A(\overline{B}{}^{0}_{s}\to\Sigma^{*+}\overline{\Sigma^{*+}}\nu\bar{\nu}),

(59)

\displaystyle A(\overline{B}{}^{0}_{s}\to\Delta^{++}\overline{\Delta^{++}}\nu\bar{\nu})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle A(\overline{B}{}^{0}_{s}\to\Delta^{+}\overline{\Delta^{+}}\nu\bar{\nu})=A(\overline{B}{}^{0}_{s}\to\Delta^{0}\overline{\Delta^{0}}\nu\bar{\nu})

(60)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle A(\overline{B}{}^{0}_{s}\to\Delta^{-}\overline{\Delta^{-}}\nu\bar{\nu}).

The above relations on amplitudes impose relations on rates.
For example, we may have three decay modes, where their rates and amplitudes are related as following

\displaystyle\Gamma_{1}=\sum_{i}|A_{1}(i)|^{2},\quad\Gamma_{2}=\sum_{i}|A_{2}(i)|^{2},\quad\Gamma_{3}=\sum_{i}|A_{1}(i)+A_{2}(i)|^{2},

(61)

with
i
representing the allowed momentum and helicities of final state particles,
summing over
i
indicating integrating over phase space and summing over final state helicities.
Note that the following discussion only applies to the SU(3) symmetric case, i.e. we are considering the relation on rates in the SU(3) symmetric limit.
Using the triangle inequality in the complex plane, we obtain

\displaystyle|A_{1}(i)|^{2}+|A_{2}(i)|^{2}-2|A_{1}(i)||A_{2}(i)|

\displaystyle\qquad\leq|A_{1}(i)+A_{2}(i)|^{2}\leq|A_{1}(i)|^{2}+|A_{2}(i)|^{2}+2|A_{1}(i)||A_{2}(i)|.

(62)

Sum over
i
in the above equation and make use of the following inequality,

\displaystyle 0\leq\sum_{i}|A_{1}(i)||A_{2}(i)|\leq\sqrt{\sum_{i}|A_{1}(i)|^{2}}\sqrt{\sum_{j}|A_{2}(j)|^{2}},

(63)

we finally obtain the triangle inequality on rates in the SU(3) symmetric limit,

\displaystyle(\Gamma_{1}^{1/2}-\Gamma_{2}^{1/2})^{2}\leq\Gamma_{3}\leq(\Gamma_{1}^{1/2}+\Gamma_{2}^{1/2})^{2}.

(64)

IV
Results on rates

Before we start the discussion on rates it will be useful to recall the detectability of the final state baryons.
In Table
9
, we identify some octet and decuplet baryons that can decay to all charged final states with unsuppressed branching ratios.
Note that modes with anti-neutron are also detectable, while

\Delta^{+}
,
\Sigma^{+,0}
,
\Xi^{0}
,
\Sigma^{*0}
and
\Xi^{*-}
can be detected by detecting a
\pi^{0}
or
\gamma
.
For example,
\Delta^{+}
mainly decays to
p\pi^{0}
and
n\pi^{+}
, while
\Sigma^{0}
decays to
\Lambda\gamma
.
We should pay close attention to the modes that involve these baryons and have large decay rates in the
\overline{B}_{q}
decays.

.

IV.1
\overline{B}_{q}\to{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}^{\prime}l\bar{\nu}
and
\overline{B}_{q}\to{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}^{\prime}\nu\bar{\nu}
decay rates

In this part, we will first give a generic discussion on
\overline{B}_{q}\to{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}^{\prime}l\bar{\nu}
and
\overline{B}_{q}\to{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}^{\prime}\nu\bar{\nu}
decays,
and the results will be compared to model calculations, where masses of hadrons and lifetimes are taken from ref.
PDG
.

For
\overline{B}_{q}\to{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}^{\prime}l\bar{\nu}
decays, the decay amplitudes can be decomposed in terms of three independent topological amplitudes, namely
T_{2{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}
,
T_{1{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}
and
A_{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}
, as shown in Table
5
.
As the amplitudes of
\overline{B}_{q}\to{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}^{\prime}l\bar{\nu}
decays have different combinations of these topological amplitudes, the corresponding branching ratios are denoted with different parameters.
Specifically,
we use

a
for the rate with
A=T_{1{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}+A_{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}
,

b
for the rate with
A=T_{2{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}
,

c
for the rate with
A=\frac{1}{2}(T_{1{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}+T_{2{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}})+A_{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}
,

d
for the rate with
A=(T_{1{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}-T_{2{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}})/2
,

e
for the rate with
A=A_{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}
,

f
for the rate with
A=\frac{1}{6}(5T_{1{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}+T_{2{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}})+A_{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}
,

g
for the rate with
A=(2T_{1{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}+T_{2{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}})/3
,
and

h
for the rate with
A=\frac{1}{3}(T_{1{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}+2T_{2{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}})+A_{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}
.
In addition, we add tildes for rates with similar amplitudes but without the
A_{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}
terms.
For example,
\tilde{a}
corresponds to the rate with
A=T_{1{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}
.
The same set of alphabets is also used in
\overline{B}_{q}\to{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}^{\prime}\nu\bar{\nu}
decays as
PB_{i\,{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}
and
PBA_{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}
are proportional to
T_{i\,{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}
and
A_{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}
with a common proportional constant
\zeta
as shown in Eq. (
23
).
Note that the above parameters correspond to the rates in the SU(3) symmetric limit.

Experimentally not only data of the branching ratio of
B^{-}\to p\bar{p}l\bar{\nu}
decay is obtained,
information of differential rate is also available.
The experimental differential rate
dBr/dm_{p\bar{p}}
of
B^{-}\to p\bar{p}\mu^{-}\bar{\nu}
decay from LHCb
LHCb:2019cgl
is shown in
Fig.
1
.
The differential rate in Fig.
1

can be well fitted with

\displaystyle\frac{dBr}{dm_{{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}^{\prime}}}=\frac{N}{(m^{2}_{{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}^{\prime}})^{\gamma}}(m_{{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}^{\prime}}-m_{\bf B}-m_{\overline{\bf B}^{\prime}}),

(65)

where
\gamma
and
N
are constants. In particular,
\gamma=9
is used in Fig.
1
for the plotted blue dashed line.
(see also Fig.
3
).
As noted in Introduction the threshold enhancement is sensitive to the position of the threshold and hence the SU(3) breaking from baryon masses are amplified producing very large SU(3) breaking effects on the integrated decay rates.

In this work we use Eq. (
65
) to estimate the SU(3) breaking effect from threshold enhancement.
Take
B^{-}\to p\bar{p}l\bar{\nu}
and
B^{-}\to\Sigma^{+}\overline{\Sigma^{+}}l\bar{\nu}
decays as examples.
As shown in Table
5
their amplitudes are both equal to
A=T_{1{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}+T_{2{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}+A_{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}
.
Consequently, without SU(3) breaking, their rates should be identical.
However, we expect large SU(3) breaking from the threshold enhancement as the masses of
p
and
\Sigma^{+}
are different. Using Eq. (
65
) the ratio of their branching ratios is given by

\displaystyle\frac{Br(B^{-}\to\Sigma^{+}\overline{\Sigma^{+}}l\bar{\nu})}{Br(B^{-}\to p\bar{p}l\bar{\nu})}=\frac{\int^{m_{B}}_{2m_{\Sigma^{+}}}dm_{{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}^{\prime}}\frac{N^{\prime}}{(m^{2}_{{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}^{\prime}})^{9}}(m_{{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}^{\prime}}-2m_{\Sigma^{+}})}{\int^{m_{B}}_{2m_{p}}dm_{{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}^{\prime}}\frac{N}{(m^{2}_{{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}^{\prime}})^{9}}(m_{{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}^{\prime}}-2m_{p})}=0.022\frac{N^{\prime}}{N}=0.022\sigma,

(66)

where we define
N^{\prime}/N\equiv\sigma
.
We see that the SU(3) breaking from the threshold enhancement is very large.
The decay rates differ by orders of magnitudes.
On the other hand, although
N^{\prime}/N=\sigma
may contain
additional SU(3) breaking from mass differences, it represents a milder SU(3) breaking effect, since the SU(3) breaking from threshold enhancement is already extracted out, the value of
\sigma
is expected to be of order one.
Consequently, using
Br(B^{-}\to p\bar{p}\mu\bar{\nu})=(5.32\pm 0.34)\times 10^{-6}

PDG
, we expect

Br(B^{-}\to\Sigma^{+}\overline{\Sigma^{+}}l\bar{\nu})=(5.32\times 0.022\sigma)\times 10^{-6}
with
\sigma
an order one parameter.
As we shall see later the above estimation agrees well with some recent theoretical calculations
Geng:2021sdl
;
Hsiao:2022uzx
.

Figure 3:

The experimental differential rate
dBr/dm_{p\bar{p}}
of
B^{-}\to p\bar{p}\mu^{-}\bar{\nu}
decay from LHCb
LHCb:2019cgl
can be well fitted with

dBr/dm_{p\bar{p}}=N(1/m^{2}_{p\bar{p}})^{9}(m_{p\bar{p}}-m_{p}-m_{\bar{p}})
with blue dashed line.
Orange and green solid lines correspond to the differential rates from Model 1 and Model 2 with inputs basically from refs
Geng:2021sdl
and
Hsiao:2022uzx
, respectively.
See text for details.

With these considerations, the branching ratios of
\overline{B}_{q}\to{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}^{\prime}l\bar{\nu}
and
\overline{B}_{q}\to{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}^{\prime}\nu\bar{\nu}
decays are parametrized and are shown in Table
10
.
SU(3) breaking effects from
B_{q}
meson widths and threshold enhancement are included.
The order one parameters

\alpha,\beta,\eta,\tilde{\eta},\tilde{\tilde{\eta}},\kappa,\tilde{\kappa},\sigma,\tilde{\sigma},\tilde{\tilde{\sigma}},\xi,\tilde{\xi},\tilde{\tilde{\xi}}

and

\bar{\alpha},\bar{\beta},\bar{\kappa},\bar{\tilde{\kappa}}
denote milder SU(3) breaking,
where different parameters are used when the baryon masses are different,
tilde are used when the combinations of topological amplitudes are different,
bar are use when the masses of baryon and anti-baryon are switched.
From the above example, we expect these parameters to be of order one. We also expect them to be of similar size, and those with bar or tilde be close to those without bar or tilde.
We will come back to these later.

decays.
The
B^{-}\to p\bar{p}l\bar{\nu}
rate is from experimental data

;

.

There are many parameters in Table
10
.
They are not totally independent, since we only have three independent topological amplitudes.
Using the triangle inequality, Eq. (
64
), the amplitude decomposition in
\overline{B}_{q}\to{\bf B}\overline{\bf B}^{\prime}l\bar{\nu}
decays and the decay rates as shown in Tables
5
and
10
,
we obtain the following inequalities,

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{\sqrt{5.32}-\sqrt{e}}{2}\Big)^{2}\lesssim

\displaystyle c

\displaystyle\lesssim\Big(\frac{\sqrt{5.32}+\sqrt{e}}{2}\Big)^{2},\quad\Big(\frac{\sqrt{5.32}-\sqrt{e}}{2}\Big)^{2}\lesssim\tilde{c}\lesssim\Big(\frac{\sqrt{5.32}+\sqrt{e}}{2}\Big)^{2},

\displaystyle(\sqrt{a}-\sqrt{e})^{2}\lesssim

\displaystyle\tilde{a}

\displaystyle\lesssim(\sqrt{a}+\sqrt{e})^{2},\quad(\sqrt{h}-\sqrt{e})^{2}\lesssim\tilde{h}\lesssim(\sqrt{h}+\sqrt{e})^{2},

(67)

\displaystyle(\sqrt{5.32}-\sqrt{a})^{2}\lesssim

\displaystyle b

\displaystyle\lesssim(\sqrt{5.32}+\sqrt{a})^{2},\quad(\sqrt{\tilde{c}}-\sqrt{\tilde{a}})^{2}\lesssim d\lesssim(\sqrt{\tilde{c}}+\sqrt{\tilde{a}})^{2},

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{\sqrt{c}-2\sqrt{a}}{3}\Big)^{2}\lesssim

\displaystyle f

\displaystyle\lesssim\Big(\frac{\sqrt{c}+2\sqrt{a}}{3}\Big)^{2},\quad\Big(\frac{2\sqrt{\tilde{c}}-\sqrt{\tilde{a}}}{3}\Big)^{2}\lesssim g\lesssim\Big(\frac{2\sqrt{\tilde{c}}+\sqrt{\tilde{a}}}{3}\Big)^{2},

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{4\sqrt{c}-\sqrt{a}}{3}\Big)^{2}\lesssim

\displaystyle h

\displaystyle\lesssim\Big(\frac{4\sqrt{c}+\sqrt{a}}{3}\Big)^{2},\quad\Big(\frac{4\sqrt{\tilde{c}}-\sqrt{\tilde{a}}}{3}\Big)^{2}\lesssim\tilde{h}\lesssim\Big(\frac{4\sqrt{\tilde{c}}+\sqrt{\tilde{a}}}{3}\Big)^{2},

(68)

and

\displaystyle(\sqrt{5.32}-\sqrt{b})^{2}\lesssim

\displaystyle a

\displaystyle\lesssim(\sqrt{5.32}+\sqrt{b})^{2},\quad(\sqrt{\tilde{c}}-\sqrt{b})^{2}\lesssim d\lesssim(\sqrt{\tilde{c}}+\sqrt{b})^{2},

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{4\sqrt{\tilde{c}}-\sqrt{b}}{3}\Big)^{2}\lesssim

\displaystyle g

\displaystyle\lesssim\Big(\frac{4\sqrt{\tilde{c}}+\sqrt{b}}{3}\Big)^{2},\quad\Big(\frac{2\sqrt{\tilde{c}}-\sqrt{b}}{3}\Big)^{2}\lesssim\tilde{h}\lesssim\Big(\frac{2\sqrt{\tilde{c}}+\sqrt{b}}{3}\Big)^{2}.

(69)

Although the above inequalities can constrain the sizes of these parameters,
it will be useful to reduce the number of the parameters.
Note that the rates proportional to
e
are governed by annihilation
A_{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}
or penguin-box-annihilation
PBA_{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}
diagrams. It is known that these contributions are usually much suppressed than tree and penguin contributions.
For example, in two-body baryonic
B_{q}
decays,
\overline{B}_{q}\to{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}^{\prime}
decays, the tree dominated mode
B^{-}\to p\bar{p}
and penguin dominated mode
B^{-}\to\Lambda\bar{p}
was observed with branching ratios at
10^{-8}
and
10^{-6}
levels, respectively
LHCb:2022lff
;
LHCb:2017swz
;
LHCb:2016nbc
, while
\overline{B}_{s}\to p\bar{p}
decay, which is an exchange and penguin-annihilation mode is not yet observed with the upper limit pushed down to
10^{-9}
level
LHCb:2022lff
.
It is therefore reasonable to consider the case where the annihilation
A_{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}
and penguin-box-annihilation
PBA_{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}
contributions are highly suppressed, i.e.
e\ll{\cal O}(1)
.
Nevertheless this assumption can be checked by searching pure annihilation (penguin-box-annuhilation) modes,
B^{-}\to\Sigma^{-}\overline{\Sigma^{-}}l\bar{\nu}
,
B^{-}\to\Xi^{-}\overline{\Xi^{-}}l\bar{\nu}
,

\overline{B}{}^{0}_{s}\to p\bar{p}\nu\bar{\nu}
and
\overline{B}{}^{0}_{s}\to n\bar{n}\nu\bar{\nu}
decays, as their rates are proportional to
e
.
In particular, as the
\overline{B}{}^{0}_{s}\to p\bar{p}\nu\bar{\nu}
mode has good detectability, see Table
9
, it is a good place to verify the above assumption.

Applying the above assumption to the relations Eq. (
71
), we obtain,

\displaystyle e\ll 5.32,\quad c\simeq\tilde{c}\simeq\frac{5.32}{4},\quad\tilde{a}\simeq a,\quad\tilde{h}\simeq h,

(70)

\displaystyle(\sqrt{5.32}-\sqrt{a})^{2}\lesssim

\displaystyle b

\displaystyle\lesssim(\sqrt{5.32}+\sqrt{a})^{2},\quad(0.5\sqrt{5.32}-\sqrt{a})^{2}\lesssim d\lesssim(0.5\sqrt{5.32}+\sqrt{a})^{2},

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{\sqrt{5.32}-4\sqrt{a}}{6}\Big)^{2}\lesssim

\displaystyle f

\displaystyle\lesssim\Big(\frac{\sqrt{5.32}+4\sqrt{a}}{6}\Big)^{2},\quad\Big(\frac{\sqrt{5.32}-\sqrt{a}}{3}\Big)^{2}\lesssim g\lesssim\Big(\frac{\sqrt{5.32}+\sqrt{a}}{3}\Big)^{2},

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{2\sqrt{5.32}-\sqrt{a}}{3}\Big)^{2}\lesssim

\displaystyle h,\tilde{h}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Big(\frac{2\sqrt{5.32}+\sqrt{a}}{3}\Big)^{2},

(71)

and

\displaystyle(\sqrt{5.32}-\sqrt{b})^{2}\lesssim

\displaystyle a

\displaystyle\lesssim(\sqrt{5.32}+\sqrt{b})^{2},\quad(0.5\sqrt{5.32}-\sqrt{b})^{2}\lesssim d\lesssim(0.5\sqrt{5.32}+\sqrt{b})^{2},

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{5\sqrt{5.32}-4\sqrt{b}}{6}\Big)^{2}\lesssim

\displaystyle f

\displaystyle\lesssim\Big(\frac{5\sqrt{5.32}+4\sqrt{b}}{6}\Big)^{2},\,\,\Big(\frac{2\sqrt{5.32}-\sqrt{b}}{3}\Big)^{2}\lesssim g\lesssim\Big(\frac{2\sqrt{5.32}+\sqrt{b}}{3}\Big)^{2},

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{\sqrt{5.32}-\sqrt{b}}{3}\Big)^{2}\lesssim

\displaystyle h,\tilde{h}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Big(\frac{\sqrt{5.32}+\sqrt{b}}{3}\Big)^{2}.

(72)

These are the inequalities we shall employed in this work.

The parameters
a
,
b
,
c
and so on in Table
10
need to satisfy the above triangular inequalities,
Eqs.(
70
), (
71
) and (
72
).
At this moment we do not have enough data to verify them.
Nevertheless, it will be useful to make use of model calculations in Sec.
II.2
for illustration.
